# Research: subset-b-000618

Grouped research for Ceph-client Linux Devicetree binding YAML schemas covering trivial devices, UFS controllers, USB controllers, USB hubs, Type-C controllers, retimers, switches, and muxes. Each source file was read completely and parsed as YAML; each section preserves the source path and is wrapped for deterministic reconciliation into source-tree-aligned per-file reports.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/trivial-devices.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/trivial-devices.yaml` defines `Trivial I2C and SPI devices`, a Trivial Devicetree device registry. This is a list of trivial I2C and SPI devices that have simple device tree bindings, consisting only of a compatible field, an address and possibly an interrupt line. If a device needs more specific bindings, such as properties to describe some aspect of it, there needs to be a specific binding document for it just like any other devices. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input; Linux does not execute this YAML at runtime, but the schema controls which DTS nodes are accepted for the corresponding hardware or bus-attached device.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The exported API is the Devicetree node contract. `$id` is `http://devicetree.org/schemas/trivial-devices.yaml#` and `$schema` is `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence and exposes 253 compatible tokens: `abb,spi-sensor`, `acbel,fsg032`, `ad,ad7414`, `adi,ad7414`, `ad,adm9240`, `adi,ad5110`, `adi,adm1027`, `adi,adt7410`, `adi,adt7411`, `adi,adt7420`, `adi,adt7422`, `adi,adt7463`, `adi,adt7468`, `adi,lt7182s`, `ams,iaq-core`, `aosong,aht10`, `aosong,aht20`, `aosong,dht20`, `arduino,unoq-mcu`, `asteralabs,pt5161l`, `atmel,atecc508a`, `atmel,atsha204`, `atmel,atsha204a`, `blutek,bpa-rs600`, `capella,cm32181`, `capella,cm3232`, `capella,cm3323`, `cisco,spi-petra`, and 225 more. Top-level properties are `reg`, `interrupts`, `spi-max-frequency`, `compatible`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties discovered in nested branches are `compatible`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none; local definitions are none. Resource/provider roles: `reg` describes MMIO windows, bus addresses, or register resources; `interrupts` maps controller, GPIO, or Type-C events into IRQ domains; `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry. Across the full schema there are 4 distinct property names and collected const/enum/min/max/item signals include `maxItems=1`, `enum=[abb,spi-sensor, acbel,fsg032, ad,ad7414, adi,ad7414, ad,adm9240, adi,ad5110, adi,adm1027, adi,adt7410, ...]`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` links, applies `properties`, `patternProperties`, required lists, item counts, const/enum checks, and composition or conditional blocks (none); it also compiles the embedded examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same rules to compiled board DTBs. During boot the kernel only consumes the DTB: OF matching selects the driver, platform/bus code maps resources, phandles are resolved, and the driver performs hardware setup.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file has no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, child-node names, phandle cell counts, register and interrupt ordering, resource names, and example topology. Runtime behavior is owned by matched drivers and hardware: there is no runtime state in this binding; each listed compatible delegates all behavior to the matched simple device driver and its bus core. Schema edits therefore persist as compatibility commitments for existing DTS files even though no code path is stored here.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Rob Herring <robh@kernel.org>. Direct schema references are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Integration points include simple I2C, SPI, GPIO, regulator, sensor, RTC, EEPROM, display, LED, and miscellaneous platform drivers that need only a stable compatible string plus common bus resources. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/crypto/atmel-ecc.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/crypto/atmel-sha204a.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/hwmon/ad7414.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/hwmon/adt7410.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/hwmon/aht10.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/hwmon/lm85.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/hwmon/occ/p8_i2c.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/hwmon/pmbus/acbel-fsg032.c`, and 2 more; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun4i-a10-topwise-a721.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun7i-a20-linutronix-testbox-v2.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a23-polaroid-mid2407pxe03.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/aspeed/aspeed-bmc-arm-stardragon4800-rep2.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/aspeed/aspeed-bmc-delta-ahe50dc.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/aspeed/aspeed-bmc-facebook-catalina.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/aspeed/aspeed-bmc-facebook-harma.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/aspeed/aspeed-bmc-facebook-minerva.dts`, and 2 more. The source was read as 531 lines of YAML with 0 embedded example blocks.

## Risks
Main risks are ABI drift around compatible-string registry churn, duplicate vendor/model entries, devices that outgrow the trivial binding and need a dedicated schema, and board nodes relying on only generic bus properties; changing compatible ordering or removing fallback strings; broadening a schema so invalid board nodes pass; tightening a schema so existing DTBs fail; and mismatches between YAML clock/reset/regulator/PHY names and driver lookup tables. Multi-role USB and Type-C bindings are especially sensitive to graph endpoints, role-switch dependencies, connector children, and wakeup semantics. UFS bindings are sensitive to per-SoC clock arrays, ICE register resources, PHY lane counts, and reset/power sequencing. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`.

## Test Signals
Use `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/trivial-devices.yaml` for this schema and its examples, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/trivial-devices.yaml` against boards using these compatibles. Useful negative tests remove each required property, scramble compatible fallback order, use bad child-node names, provide too many/few clocks, resets, interrupts, PHYs, or register windows, and add undeclared properties when the schema is closed. Runtime signals are clean driver probe, expected PHY/role-switch/provider registration where applicable, correct device enumeration, suspend/resume without resource leaks, and no new schema warnings.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/trivial-devices.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/ufs/qcom,ufs.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/ufs/qcom,ufs.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Universal Flash Storage (UFS) Controller`, a Qualcomm UFS host-controller binding. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input; Linux does not execute this YAML at runtime, but the schema controls which DTS nodes are accepted for the corresponding hardware or bus-attached device.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The exported API is the Devicetree node contract. `$id` is `http://devicetree.org/schemas/ufs/qcom,ufs.yaml#` and `$schema` is `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence and exposes 10 compatible tokens: `qcom,msm8994-ufshc`, `qcom,msm8996-ufshc`, `qcom,qcs615-ufshc`, `qcom,sdm845-ufshc`, `qcom,sm6115-ufshc`, `qcom,sm6125-ufshc`, `qcom,sm6350-ufshc`, `qcom,sm8150-ufshc`, `qcom,ufshc`, `jedec,ufs-2.0`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `qcom,ice`, `reg`, `reg-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties discovered in nested branches are `compatible`, `qcom,ice`, `reg`, `reg-names`. Patterned child schemas are none; local definitions are none. Resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO windows, bus addresses, or register resources; `reg-names` fixes semantic ordering for multiple register windows. Across the full schema there are 6 distinct property names and collected const/enum/min/max/item signals include `enum=[qcom,msm8994-ufshc, qcom,msm8996-ufshc, qcom,qcs615-ufshc, qcom,sdm845-ufshc, qcom,sm6115-ufshc, qcom,sm6125-ufshc, qcom,sm6350-ufshc, qcom,sm8150-ufshc]`, `const=qcom,ufshc`, `const=jedec,ufs-2.0`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=2`, `const=std`, `const=ice`, `enum=[qcom,sdm845-ufshc, qcom,sm6350-ufshc, qcom,sm8150-ufshc]`, `minItems=9`, `maxItems=9`, `const=core_clk`, `const=bus_aggr_clk`, `const=iface_clk`, `const=core_clk_unipro`, `const=ref_clk`, `const=tx_lane0_sync_clk`, `const=rx_lane0_sync_clk`, `const=rx_lane1_sync_clk`, `const=ice_core_clk`, `minItems=2`, `enum=[qcom,msm8996-ufshc]`, `const=bus_clk`, `const=core_clk_ice`, `maxItems=1`, and 4 more.

## Control Flow
Control flow is schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` links, applies `properties`, `patternProperties`, required lists, item counts, const/enum checks, and composition or conditional blocks (`select`, `allOf`); it also compiles the embedded examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same rules to compiled board DTBs. During boot the kernel only consumes the DTB: OF matching selects the driver, platform/bus code maps resources, phandles are resolved, and the driver performs hardware setup.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file has no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, child-node names, phandle cell counts, register and interrupt ordering, resource names, and example topology. Runtime behavior is owned by matched drivers and hardware: UFS link bring-up, UniPro/MPHY power mode, runtime PM, clock scaling, inline crypto setup, reset sequencing, error recovery, and host controller register state. Schema edits therefore persist as compatibility commitments for existing DTS files even though no code path is stored here.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Bjorn Andersson <bjorn.andersson@linaro.org>, Andy Gross <agross@kernel.org>. Direct schema references are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `qcom,ufs-common.yaml`. Integration points include the Linux UFSHCD core, Qualcomm UFS host driver, UFS PHY phandles, clocks, resets, interconnects, ICE crypto engine resources, regulators, IOMMU, power domains, and board storage nodes. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ufs/host/ufs-qcom.c`; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/eliza.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/hamoa.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/kaanapali.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/kodiak.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/lemans.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/milos.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/monaco.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8996.dtsi`, and 2 more. The source was read as 240 lines of YAML with 1 embedded example block.

## Risks
Main risks are ABI drift around compatible fallback order, clock-name arrays, `reg`/`reg-names` ICE windows, `qcom,ice` phandles, reset lines, PHY linkage, and per-SoC conditional constraints; changing compatible ordering or removing fallback strings; broadening a schema so invalid board nodes pass; tightening a schema so existing DTBs fail; and mismatches between YAML clock/reset/regulator/PHY names and driver lookup tables. Multi-role USB and Type-C bindings are especially sensitive to graph endpoints, role-switch dependencies, connector children, and wakeup semantics. UFS bindings are sensitive to per-SoC clock arrays, ICE register resources, PHY lane counts, and reset/power sequencing. The closure profile closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`.

## Test Signals
Use `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/ufs/qcom,ufs.yaml` for this schema and its examples, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/ufs/qcom,ufs.yaml` against boards using these compatibles. Useful negative tests remove each required property, scramble compatible fallback order, use bad child-node names, provide too many/few clocks, resets, interrupts, PHYs, or register windows, and add undeclared properties when the schema is closed. Runtime signals are clean driver probe, expected PHY/role-switch/provider registration where applicable, correct device enumeration, suspend/resume without resource leaks, and no new schema warnings. Example seed: #include <dt-bindings/clock/qcom,gcc-sm8150.h>; #include <dt-bindings/clock/qcom,rpmh.h>; #include <dt-bindings/gpio/gpio.h>; #include <dt-bindings/interconnect/qcom,sm8150.h>; #include <dt-bindings/interrupt-controller/arm-gic.h>; ; soc {; #address-cells =...

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/amlogic,meson-g12a-usb-ctrl.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/amlogic,meson-g12a-usb-ctrl.yaml` defines `Amlogic Meson G12A DWC3 USB SoC Controller Glue`, a Synopsys DesignWare USB3 glue-controller binding. The Amlogic G12A embeds a DWC3 USB IP Core configured for USB2 and USB3 in host-only mode, and a DWC2 IP Core configured for USB2 peripheral mode only. A glue connects the DWC3 core to USB2 PHYs and optionally to an USB3 PHY. One of the USB2 PHYs can be re-routed in peripheral mode to a DWC2 USB IP. The DWC3 Glue controls the PHY routing and power, an interrupt line is connected to the Glue to serve as OTG ID change detection. The Amlogic A1 embeds a DWC3 USB IP Core configured for USB2 in host-only mode. The Amlogic GXL, GXM & AXG SoCs doesn't embed an USB3 PHY. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input; Linux does not execute this YAML at runtime, but the schema controls which DTS nodes are accepted for the corresponding hardware or bus-attached device.

Top-level properties are `compatible`, `ranges`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `resets`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `phy-names`, `phys`, `dr_mode`, `power-domains`, `vbus-supply`. Patterned child schemas are `^usb@[0-9a-f]+$`; local definitions are none. Resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `ranges` controls child-node address translation and bus layout; `#address-cells` controls child-node address translation and bus layout; `#size-cells` controls child-node address translation and bus layout; `clocks` integrates with the clock framework; `clock-names` integrates with the clock framework; `resets` integrates with reset-controller providers or consumers; `reg` describes MMIO windows, bus addresses, or register resources; `interrupts` maps controller, GPIO, or Type-C events into IRQ domains; `phy-names` connects USB/UFS controllers to PHY providers or exposes a PHY provider; `phys` connects USB/UFS controllers to PHY providers or exposes a PHY provider; `dr_mode` models USB dual-role, Type-C, or graph topology; `power-domains` models generic power-domain dependencies; `vbus-supply` connects regulator supplies. Across the full schema there are 14 distinct property names and collected const/enum/min/max/item signals include `enum=[amlogic,meson-gxl-usb-ctrl, amlogic,meson-gxm-usb-ctrl, amlogic,meson-axg-usb-ctrl, amlogic,meson-g12a-usb-ctrl, amlogic,meson-a1-usb-ctrl]`, `enum=[1, 2]`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=3`, `maxItems=1`, `enum=[amlogic,meson-g12a-usb-ctrl]`, `minItems=2`, `const=usb2-phy0`, `const=usb2-phy1`, `const=usb3-phy0`, `enum=[amlogic,meson-gxl-usb-ctrl]`, `const=usb_ctrl`, `const=ddr`, `enum=[amlogic,meson-gxm-usb-ctrl]`, `const=usb2-phy2`, `enum=[amlogic,meson-axg-usb-ctrl]`, `enum=[amlogic,meson-a1-usb-ctrl]`, `minItems=3`, `const=usb_bus`, `const=xtal_usb_ctrl`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` links, applies `properties`, `patternProperties`, required lists, item counts, const/enum checks, and composition or conditional blocks (`allOf`); it also compiles the embedded examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same rules to compiled board DTBs. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/aspeed,usb-vhub.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/aspeed,usb-vhub.yaml` defines `ASPEED USB 2.0 Virtual Hub Controller`, a USB hub binding. The ASPEED USB 2.0 Virtual Hub Controller implements 1 set of USB Hub register and several sets of Device and Endpoint registers to support the Virtual Hub's downstream USB devices. Supported number of devices and endpoints vary depending on hardware revisions. AST2400 and AST2500 Virtual Hub supports 5 downstream devices and 15 generic endpoints, while AST2600 and AST2700 Virtual Hub supports 7 downstream devices and 21 generic endpoints. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input; Linux does not execute this YAML at runtime, but the schema controls which DTS nodes are accepted for the corresponding hardware or bus-attached device.

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/ci-hdrc-usb2.yaml` defines `USB2 ChipIdea USB controller`, a USB2 OTG/host/device controller binding. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `operating-points-v2`, `phy-select`, `nvidia,phy`, `nvidia,needs-double-reset`, `ulpi`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`; required properties discovered in nested branches are `compatible`. Patterned child schemas are none; local definitions are none. Resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `clocks` integrates with the clock framework; `clock-names` integrates with the clock framework. Across the full schema there are 8 distinct property names and collected const/enum/min/max/item signals include `enum=[chipidea,usb2, lsi,zevio-usb, nuvoton,npcm750-udc, nvidia,tegra20-ehci, nvidia,tegra20-udc, nvidia,tegra30-ehci, nvidia,tegra30-udc, nvidia,tegra114-udc, ...]`, `enum=[nvidia,tegra114-ehci, nvidia,tegra124-ehci, nvidia,tegra210-ehci]`, `const=nvidia,tegra30-ehci`, `const=xlnx,zynq-usb-2.20a`, `const=chipidea,usb2`, `enum=[nuvoton,npcm845-udc]`, `const=nuvoton,npcm750-udc`, `enum=[nxp,s32g3-usb]`, `const=nxp,s32g2-usb`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=2`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` links, applies `properties`, `patternProperties`, required lists, item counts, const/enum checks, and composition or conditional blocks (`allOf`); it also compiles the embedded examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same rules to compiled board DTBs. During boot the kernel only consumes the DTB: OF matching selects the driver, platform/bus code maps resources, phandles are resolved, and the driver performs hardware setup.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file has no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, child-node names, phandle cell counts, register and interrupt ordering, resource names, and example topology. Runtime behavior is owned by matched drivers and hardware: USB host/peripheral/OTG mode, endpoint FIFOs, DMA queues, VBUS/ID detection, suspend/resume wakeup, PHY power, and controller reset state. Schema edits therefore persist as compatibility commitments for existing DTS files even though no code path is stored here.

The file has no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. ## Risks
Main risks are ABI drift around compatible fallback order, MMIO and IRQ resources, PHY/transceiver phandles, `dr_mode`, FIFO/endpoint tuning, VBUS/ID GPIOs, clocks/resets, and child-port layout; changing compatible ordering or removing fallback strings; broadening a schema so invalid board nodes pass; tightening a schema so existing DTBs fail; and mismatches between YAML clock/reset/regulator/PHY names and driver lookup tables. Multi-role USB and Type-C bindings are especially sensitive to graph endpoints, role-switch dependencies, connector children, and wakeup semantics. UFS bindings are sensitive to per-SoC clock arrays, ICE register resources, PHY lane counts, and reset/power sequencing. The closure profile closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`.

## Test Signals
Use `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/dwc2.yaml` for this schema and its examples, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/dwc2.yaml` against boards using these compatibles. Useful negative tests remove each required property, scramble compatible fallback order, use bad child-node names, provide too many/few clocks, resets, interrupts, PHYs, or register windows, and add undeclared properties when the schema is closed. Runtime signals are clean driver probe, expected PHY/role-switch/provider registration where applicable, correct device enumeration, suspend/resume without resource leaks, and no new schema warnings. Example seed: usb@101c0000 {; compatible = "rockchip,rk3066-usb", "snps,dwc2";; reg = <0x10180000 0x40000>;; interrupts = <18>;; clocks = <&usb_otg_ahb_clk>;; clock-names = "otg";; phys = <&usbphy>;; phy-names = "usb2-phy";; };;

`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/fcs,fsa4480.yaml` defines `ON Semiconductor Analog Audio Switch`, a USB Type-C controller, mux, retimer, or switch binding. ## Control Flow

The file has no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, child-node names, phandle cell counts, register and interrupt ordering, resource names, and example topology. Runtime behavior is owned by matched drivers and hardware: Type-C attach/orientation state, CC/PD negotiation, role swaps, alt-mode mux state, HPD/IRQ handling, and retimer/switch programming state. ## Control Flow
Control flow is schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` links, applies `properties`, `patternProperties`, required lists, item counts, const/enum checks, and composition or conditional blocks (`allOf`); it also compiles the embedded examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same rules to compiled board DTBs. During boot the kernel only consumes the DTB: OF matching selects the driver, platform/bus code maps resources, phandles are resolved, and the driver performs hardware setup.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file has no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, child-node names, phandle cell counts, register and interrupt ordering, resource names, and example topology. Runtime behavior is owned by matched drivers and hardware: host controller schedule state, root-hub status, port power/connect state, DMA access, runtime PM, and wakeup state. Schema edits therefore persist as compatibility commitments for existing DTS files even though no code path is stored here.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Greg Kroah-Hartman <gregkh@linuxfoundation.org>. Direct schema references are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/flag`, `usb-hcd.yaml`. Integration points include the EHCI/OHCI host stack, platform HCD glue drivers, clocks, resets, PHYs, regulators, interrupts, companion controllers, and board USB-port topology. The source was read as 212 lines of YAML with 2 embedded example blocks.

## Risks
Main risks are ABI drift around compatible fallback strings, MMIO window and IRQ ordering, clock/reset lists, PHY references, companion-controller relationships, and port-power controls; changing compatible ordering or removing fallback strings; broadening a schema so invalid board nodes pass; tightening a schema so existing DTBs fail; and mismatches between YAML clock/reset/regulator/PHY names and driver lookup tables. Multi-role USB and Type-C bindings are especially sensitive to graph endpoints, role-switch dependencies, connector children, and wakeup semantics. UFS bindings are sensitive to per-SoC clock arrays, ICE register resources, PHY lane counts, and reset/power sequencing. The closure profile closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`.

## Test Signals
Use `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/generic-ehci.yaml` for this schema and its examples, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/generic-ehci.yaml` against boards using these compatibles. Useful negative tests remove each required property, scramble compatible fallback order, use bad child-node names, provide too many/few clocks, resets, interrupts, PHYs, or register windows, and add undeclared properties when the schema is closed. Runtime signals are clean driver probe, expected PHY/role-switch/provider registration where applicable, correct device enumeration, suspend/resume without resource leaks, and no new schema warnings. Example seed: usb@e0000300 {; compatible = "ibm,usb-ehci-440epx", "usb-ehci";; interrupt-parent = <&UIC0>;; interrupts = <0x1a 4>;; reg = <0xe0000300 90>, <0xe0000390 70>;; big-endian;; };;

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/microchip,usb5744.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/microchip,usb5744.yaml` defines `Microchip USB5744 4-port Hub Controller`, a USB hub binding. Microchip's USB5744 SmartHubTM IC is a 4 port, SuperSpeed (SS)/Hi-Speed (HS), low power, low pin count configurable and fully compliant with the USB 3.1 Gen 1 specification. The USB5744 also supports Full Speed (FS) and Low Speed (LS) USB signaling, offering complete coverage of all defined USB operating speeds. The new SuperSpeed hubs operate in parallel with the USB 2.0 controller, so 5 Gbps SuperSpeed data transfers are not affected by slower USB 2.0 traffic. ## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/nvidia,tegra234-xusb.yaml` defines `NVIDIA Tegra234 xHCI controller`, a USB peripheral/support binding. `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/usb/nxp,ptn5110.yaml` defines `NXP PTN5110 Type-C Port Controller`, a USB Type-C controller, mux, retimer, or switch binding. It is declarative firmware ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input; Linux does not execute this YAML at runtime, but the schema controls which DTS nodes are accepted for the corresponding hardware or bus-attached device.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The exported API is the Devicetree node contract. `$id` is `http://devicetree.org/schemas/usb/nxp,ptn5110.yaml#` and `$schema` is `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence and exposes 2 compatible tokens: `nxp,ptn5110`, `tcpci`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `connector`, `orientation-gpios`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `connector`; required properties discovered in nested branches are `compatible`, `connector`, `interrupts`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none; local definitions are none. Resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO windows, bus addresses, or register resources; `interrupts` maps controller, GPIO, or Type-C events into IRQ domains; `connector` models USB dual-role, Type-C, or graph topology; `orientation-gpios` describes GPIO control/status wiring. Across the full schema there are 5 distinct property names and collected const/enum/min/max/item signals include `const=nxp,ptn5110`, `const=tcpci`, `maxItems=1`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` parses the YAML, validates the meta-schema, resolves `$ref` links, applies `properties`, `patternProperties`, required lists, item counts, const/enum checks, and composition or conditional blocks (none); it also compiles the embedded examples. `dtbs_check` applies the same rules to compiled board DTBs. During boot the kernel only consumes the DTB: OF matching selects the driver, platform/bus code maps resources, phandles are resolved, and the driver performs hardware setup.

## State and Persistence Behavior
The file has no mutable runtime state and performs no persistence. Its durable state is ABI shape: compatible strings, property names, child-node names, phandle cell counts, register and interrupt ordering, resource names, and example topology. Runtime behavior is owned by matched drivers and hardware: Type-C attach/orientation state, CC/PD negotiation, role swaps, alt-mode mux state, HPD/IRQ handling, and retimer/switch programming state. Schema edits therefore persist as compatibility commitments for existing DTS files even though no code path is stored here.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Li Jun <jun.li@nxp.com>. Direct schema references are `/schemas/connector/usb-connector.yaml#`. Integration points include the Linux Type-C class, TCPM/TCPCI, USB role-switch, extcon or connector graph links, I2C client drivers, GPIO controls, interrupts, regulators, DisplayPort alt-mode routing, and orientation/super-speed mux paths. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/max77759_charger.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/usb/typec/anx7411.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/usb/typec/tcpm/Kconfig`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/usb/typec/tcpm/Makefile`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/usb/typec/tcpm/maxim_contaminant.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/usb/typec/tcpm/tcpci.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/usb/typec/tcpm/tcpci_maxim.h`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/usb/typec/tcpm/tcpci_maxim_core.c`, and 2 more; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/exynos/google/gs101-pixel-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mm-evk.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mn-evk.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mp-beacon-kit.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mp-ultra-mach-sbc.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mq-hummingboard-pulse.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mq-librem5-devkit.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8qm-mek.dts`, and 2 more.
